```sh
cd /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-adamure-Development-Femmli-Half-Life/e929ff11-d161-4d77-a7dd-9ff08c011566/scratchpad/work && python3 - <<'EOF'
import re, json
p='Half-Life/Localizable.xcstrings'
t=open(p).read()
old_pref='''    "Prefer not to say" : {
      "comment" : "Onboarding's About you step: the age picker's choice for not giving an age."
    },'''
new_pref='''    "Prefer not to say" : {
      "comment" : "About you, in onboarding and Settings: the option under the date-of-birth picker for not giving a date of birth."
    },'''
assert t.count(old_pref)==1; t=t.replace(old_pref,new_pref)
m=re.search(r'    "Age" : \{\n      "comment" : "[^"]*"\n    \},', t); assert m
t=t.replace(m.group(0), '''    "Age" : {
      "comment" : "About you, in onboarding and Settings: the heading over the date-of-birth picker and its \\"Prefer not to say\\" option."
    },''')
assert '"Date of birth" : {' not in t
keys=[(mm.start(), mm.group(1)) for mm in re.finditer(r'^    "((?:[^"\\]|\\.)*)" : \{', t, re.M)]
pos=next(s for s,k in keys if k.lower() > "date of birth")
block='''    "Date of birth" : {
      "comment" : "About you, in onboarding and Settings: the label of the date-of-birth picker."
    },
'''
t=t[:pos]+block+t[pos:]
open(p,'w').write(t)
json.loads(t); print("catalog ok")
for f,old,new in [('Half-Life/Domain/UseCases/ObserveRecommendedSleepUseCase.swift','/// - Parameter input: The calendar whose year the user\'s age is counted in.','/// - Parameter input: The calendar whose time zone the user\'s age is counted in.'),('Half-Life/Data/Repositories/LiveUserProfileRepository.swift','/// - Parameter calendar: The calendar whose year the user\'s age is counted in.','/// - Parameter calendar: The calendar whose time zone the user\'s age is counted in.')]:
    s=open(f).read(); assert s.count(old)==1, f; open(f,'w').write(s.replace(old,new)); print("fixed", f)
EOF

148	
149	| Rule | Calculates | Executed by |
150	|------|------------|-------------|
151	| `HalfLifePriorRule` | The starting half-life, from the factors | ``UserProfileRepository``, when the factors are saved |
152	| `SleepNeedRule` | The recommended sleep range, from the birth year and the current date | ``UserProfileRepository`` |
153	
154	#### HalfLifePriorRule
155	
156	The starting half-life is the standard 5.5 hours times each factor's multiplier:
157	
158	```
159	T½ = 5.5 h × m(pregnancy or estrogen) × m(smokes) × m(cirrhosis) × m(fluvoxamine)
160	```
161	
162	- **Pregnancy and estrogen don't stack.** Both slow the same enzyme, so when both are chosen, only the larger multiplier applies.
163	- **The rest multiply.** Each one changes clearance independently, so their effects on the half-life multiply.
164	- **The result is clamped to 3 to 40 hours.** Smoking alone gives 3.3 hours. Fluvoxamine alone gives 33 hours, and it has been measured at 56. Above 40 hours the model's other uncertainties dwarf the half-life's, and intakes would keep counting for more than two weeks (<doc:CaffeineDecayModel>). This range is the one the Caffeine Decay Model article left to the tuning features.
165	
166	| Factor | Multiplier | Starting half-life | Evidence |
167	|--------|-----------:|-------------------:|----------|
168	| None | ×1.0 | 5.5 h | — |
169	| Pregnant, first trimester | ×1.5 | 8.3 h | Caffeine clearance was 33% lower at 14–18 weeks than after the birth (Tracy 2005, 25 women followed through pregnancy). No primary study measured the first trimester itself. This is an upper bound, which errs toward an earlier cutoff. |
170	| Pregnant, second trimester | ×1.9 | 10.5 h | 48% lower at 24–28 weeks (Tracy 2005) |
171	| Pregnant, third trimester | ×2.7 | 14.9 h | 65% lower at 36–40 weeks (Tracy 2005, ×2.9). Clearance at term was 39% of normal (Parsons 1982, ×2.5). The half-life was 10.5 hours in the last 4 weeks, against 3.4 hours in women who weren't pregnant (Knutti 1981). |
172	| Estrogen | ×1.5 | 8.3 h | Low-dose combined pills: 7.9 hours against 5.4 (Abernethy 1985, 9 users and 9 matched controls). Older and other pills measured ×1.7 to ×2.2 (Patwardhan 1980, Balogh 1995). Hormone therapy measured about ×1.4 (Pollock 1999). The patch and the ring haven't been studied. They contain the same estrogen, so they're assumed to act the same. |
173	| Smokes | ×0.6 | 3.3 h | 3.5 hours against 6.0 (Parsons 1978, 13 and 13). Clearance falls 36% after quitting (Faber 2004). A nicotine patch changed nothing (Hukkanen 2011, randomized crossover). |
174	| Cirrhosis | ×2.5 | 13.8 h | 13.7 hours against 3.8 (Renner 1984, ×3.6), but only ×1.2 in a milder group (Desmond 1980). The effect depends on severity, so this is between the two. |
175	| Fluvoxamine | ×6.0 | 33 h | 31 hours against 5 (Jeppesen 1996, 8 people, ×6.2). Another study measured 56 hours against 4.9 (Culm-Merdek 2005, ×11). The FDA lists it as the one strong inhibitor of the enzyme that clears caffeine. |
176	
177	Each multiplier is a starting point for one person, drawn from group averages. Healthy people alone vary from about 2.3 to 9.9 hours (Blanchard 1983, 16 men), so the step and the summary call the result a starting estimate. The personal half-life estimator (rank 9) starts from it and updates it with the user's own nights. When the nights can't say anything, the estimate stays at this starting value (<doc:HalfLifeEstimator>).
178	
179	**Considered and left out.** Each of these was in the research, and each fell short:
180	
181	| Factor | Why it's left out |
182	|--------|-------------------|
183	| Age | No significant difference between men of 71 and 20 (Blanchard 1983). The owner decided the same on 2026-09-12. |
184	| Progestin-only pills, implants, and IUDs | No caffeine study found. The estrogen option's copy says "with estrogen" so these users don't choose it. |
185	| Vaping, nicotine patches, and heated tobacco | Nicotine doesn't speed up clearance (Hukkanen 2011). Switching from cigarettes behaves like quitting (van der Plas 2020). |
186	| Fatty liver and hepatitis, without cirrhosis | No caffeine study found. |
187	| Ciprofloxacin (×1.5) and enoxacin (×6) | Antibiotic courses last days, so a stored answer would be wrong within a week or two. Enoxacin is rarely prescribed. |

124	### Saving as the user goes
125	
126	Settings follows the app's one-way data flow (constitution Article I.5), as onboarding does.
127	
128	- **The factors follow the repository.** An option saves at once, and the section changes only when the repository publishes the saved factors.
129	- **The name, age, and bedtime are the form's own editing state** (Article I.2). They're filled from every profile the repository publishes, with two exceptions. A name being typed isn't replaced until it's committed. And while a save is under way, a published profile doesn't move any field, so a picker being turned doesn't jump back to a value that was just replaced.
130	- **The name saves when it's committed**, when the user presses Return or leaves the field, not on every keystroke. A name that wasn't changed saves nothing. The field wraps, as onboarding's does, because the audit flagged a one-line field as clippable. A wrapping field types a newline on Return instead of submitting, so the reducer takes a newline as the commit, and removes it.
131	- **The age and the bedtime save as soon as they're chosen.** The age saves with the name as it's shown, because the two are saved together (``SaveAboutYouUseCase``).
132	- **A failed save puts the fields back** to what the repository last published, and the failure is logged. The factors never changed in the first place.
133	
134	### Demo data
135	
136	- The section's button reads "Add 30 days of demo drinks" while the log holds none, and "Remove demo drinks" while it does. Which one shows follows the repository's answer, not the tap.
137	- While a change is under way, the button is disabled, and another tap does nothing.
138	- A failed change says so under the button, "The demo drinks couldn't be changed. Try again.", until the next try.
139	- The demo Health data switch is an option under the demo drinks' card, with its own failure message (<doc:AppleHealthCard>).
140	
141	## Navigation
142	
143	Settings is the second tab in ``AppView``'s system tab bar. The log button stays above the bar on both tabs.
144	
145	### Settings' own screens
146	
147	Settings is hierarchical. ``SettingsFeature`` holds a `StackState` path, as onboarding does (constitution Article I.6), and ``SettingsView`` shows it in a `NavigationStack`:
148	
149	| Row | Its value on the root | The screen it pushes | The screen's feature |
150	|-----|-----------------------|----------------------|----------------------|
151	| About you | The name, when there is one | ``AboutYouSettingsView`` | ``ProfileSettingsFeature`` |
152	| Caffeine and your body | None. The half-life isn't shown (see "The owner's decisions"). | ``FactorsSettingsView`` | ``ProfileSettingsFeature`` |
153	| Bedtime | The bedtime, such as "10:30 PM" | ``BedtimeSettingsView`` | ``ProfileSettingsFeature`` |
154	| Permissions | None | ``PermissionSettingsView`` | ``PermissionsFeature`` |
155	| App lock | "On" or "Off" | ``AppLockSettingsView``, around ``AppLockSettingsSection`` | ``AppLockSettingsFeature`` |
156	| Demo data | "Added" while the log holds demo drinks | ``DemoHistorySettingsView`` | ``DemoDataFeature``, which runs ``DemoHistoryFeature`` and ``DemoHealthDataFeature`` |
157	
158	- **Tapping a row sends `rowTapped`, and the reducer pushes the row's screen** with fresh state (SET-3). The screen observes its own data, as each section did on the flat tab, so it shows the saved values as soon as it opens.
159	- **The root keeps three sections of its own**, for its rows' values: the profile, the app lock, and the demo drinks. Each follows its repository, so a change made on a pushed screen shows on the root when the user goes back.
160	- **The three profile screens share ``ProfileSettingsFeature``**, which already handles the name, age, factors, and bedtime. Each pushed screen has its own instance, and shows only its own part.
161	- **Each screen has its own view, identifier file, and robot** (constitution Articles II.5 and II.7). Its `screen` identifier is on its scroll view, which is also what its robot swipes. A container's identifier given to its only child replaced the child's own identifier, so no screen has a separate one for its scroll view.
162	- **Each pushed screen is laid out like onboarding's steps**: a large title on the solid page, its section's cards, and the navigation stack's own back button, so swiping back works too.
163	
```

**15:55:54 · Tool call: Edit**

- `replace_all`: `false`
- `file_path`: `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-adamure-Development-Femmli-Half-Life/e929ff11-d161-4d77-a7dd-9ff08c011566/scratchpad/work/Half-Life/Documentation.docc/Onboarding.md`
- `old_string`:

```
- **The age is stored as a birth year**, the current year minus the age given, so it doesn't go stale. It can be a year off, which matters only in the year a user crosses a sleep range's boundary. The owner chose this on 2026-09-12.

### HalfLifeFactor
```
- `new_string`:

```
- **The age is stored as a date of birth**, so it doesn't go stale, and it's exact. From 2026-09-12 it was a birth year, the current year minus the age given, which could be a year off in the year a user crossed a sleep range's boundary. The owner chose the date of birth on 2026-09-13, when the age wheel became a date picker.

### DateOfBirth

The day the user was born: a year, month, and day in the Gregorian calendar, with no time of day or time zone. It's always a real day. Because it's a day, not a moment, it stays the same day when the user travels or changes calendar. Added on 2026-09-13.

- `init(_:in:)` takes the day that a date picker's moment falls on, in the user's time zone, and `date(in:)` gives back the start of that day, for the picker to show.
- `age(at:in:)` counts the whole years to a moment, in the user's time zone, so the age goes up at the start of the birthday.

### HalfLifeFactor
```

- `old_string`:

```
| SETPROF-1 | The profile sections show the saved profile, refilled from every profile the repository publishes. The age fills in once the current year is known, and a saved age outside the picker is left empty. |
| SETPROF-2 | The name saves when it's committed, with the age. A newline typed in the name commits it, without the newline. A name being typed isn't replaced by a published profile, and a name that wasn't changed saves nothing. |
| SETPROF-3 | The age and the bedtime save as soon as they're chosen. |
| SETPROF-4 | A profile published while a save is under way doesn't move the name, age, or bedtime. |
| SETPROF-5 | A failed save puts the name, age, and bedtime back to what was last published. |
```
- `new_string`:

```
| SETPROF-1 | The profile sections show the saved profile, refilled from every profile the repository publishes. The date of birth fills in once the picker's dates are known, and a saved date of birth the picker doesn't offer is left empty. Until 2026-09-13 it was an age, filled in once the current year was known. |
| SETPROF-2 | The name saves when it's committed, with the date of birth. A newline typed in the name commits it, without the newline. A name being typed isn't replaced by a published profile, and a name that wasn't changed saves nothing. |
| SETPROF-3 | The date of birth and the bedtime save as soon as they're chosen. "Prefer not to say" clears the date of birth and saves, unless no date was chosen, when it saves nothing. |
| SETPROF-4 | A profile published while a save is under way doesn't move the name, date of birth, or bedtime. |
| SETPROF-5 | A failed save puts the name, date of birth, and bedtime back to what was last published. |
```

416	| ``ObserveRecommendedSleepUseCase`` | Streams the sleep recommended for the user's age, in a given calendar | ``UserProfileRepository`` |
417	| ``ObservePermissionsUseCase`` | Streams the Health, notification, and Face ID permissions | ``PermissionsRepository`` |
418	| ``RequestHealthAccessUseCase``, ``RequestNotificationPermissionUseCase``, ``RequestBiometricPermissionUseCase`` | Each asks for its permission | ``PermissionsRepository`` |
419	| ``RefreshPermissionsUseCase`` | Re-reads the permissions, for example after the Settings app | ``PermissionsRepository`` |
420	| ``OpenAppSettingsUseCase`` | Opens Half-Life's page in the Settings app | ``PermissionsRepository`` |
421	| ``ObserveAppLockUseCase`` | Streams the app lock: whether it's on, and whether the app is locked (<doc:AppLock>) | ``AppLockRepository`` |
422	| ``TurnOnAppLockUseCase`` | Asks for Face ID if it hasn't been asked for, then turns the app lock on if it's allowed | ``PermissionsRepository``, ``AppLockRepository`` |
423	| ``TurnOffAppLockUseCase``, ``LockAppUseCase``, ``UnlockAppUseCase`` | Turn the app lock off, lock the app if the lock is on, and ask the user to unlock it | ``AppLockRepository`` |
424	| ``ObserveLanguageModelAvailabilityUseCase`` | Streams whether the on-device language model can be used, so features that use it hide while it can't (<doc:LanguageModel>) | ``LanguageModelRepository`` |
425	| ``RespondToInstructionUseCase`` | Answers one instruction with the on-device language model, in a session of its own. Only an App Intent's instruction can log a drink (<doc:LanguageModel>). | ``LanguageModelRepository`` |
426	| ``WriteInsightUseCase`` | Puts the Insights tab's first finding into words with the on-device language model, from the finding the card holds and the one the user last disagreed with (<doc:Insights>) | ``LanguageModelRepository`` |
427	| ``KeepWidgetsCurrentUseCase`` | Keeps the widget snapshot current for as long as it runs, from launch (<doc:Widgets>) | ``WidgetSnapshotRepository`` |
428	| ``ObserveWidgetTimelineUseCase`` | Streams the widgets' timeline, in a given calendar, for the widget extension (<doc:Widgets>) | ``WidgetTimelineRepository`` |
429	
430	## Business rules
431	
432	| Business rule | Calculates | Executed by |
433	|---------------|------------|-------------|
434	| ``CaffeineDecayRule`` | The active caffeine curve under Bateman absorption, which intakes count and which are negligible, and when an intake is half gone (<doc:CaffeineDecayModel>) | ``LiveCaffeineDecayRepository`` |
435	| ``CaffeineCutoffRule`` | The cutoff: the latest time, rounded down to the half hour, when the user's usual drink, on top of every intake logged, leaves no more than the sleep threshold at the next bedtime. It only considers cups that peak by bedtime, and asks ``CaffeineDecayRule`` for the levels (<doc:CaffeineCutoff>). It also gives the cutoffs for several nights, each calculated from the bedtime before it (<doc:CutoffReminder>), and the drink composer's warning for a drink past its cutoff (<doc:CaffeineCutoff>). | ``LiveCaffeineDecayRepository``, which also runs ``FavouriteDrinksRule`` to find the usual drink |
436	| ``SleepWindowRule`` | Tonight's sleep window: the first whole minute after which the caffeine logged stays at or below the sleep threshold until noon, and the 90 minutes from the later of that and the bedtime. It asks ``CaffeineDecayRule`` for the level at every minute from 6pm (<doc:Insights>). | ``LiveCaffeineDecayRepository`` |
437	| ``SleepHistoryRule`` | The night that followed each of the last several days: ``LastNightSleepRule``'s night ending from noon that day to noon the next, with today's always empty, and the one range to read sleep for (<doc:Insights>) | ``LiveHealthDataRepository`` |
438	| ``HealthDataAvailabilityRule`` | Which kinds of Health data have any data: sleep when any sleep was recorded, and steps and resting heart rate when any day has a value (<doc:Insights>) | ``LiveHealthDataRepository`` |
439	| ``CaffeineNightRule`` | Each past night's caffeine, for every comparison on the Insights tab: at the sleep onset Health recorded, the first from noon that day to noon the next, or else at the bedtime, and whether it's over the sleep threshold (<doc:Insights>) | ``SleepToleranceRepository``, for `caffeineNights(days:in:)` |
440	| ``SleepToleranceRule`` | The Sleep screen's analysis: the nights of the last 30 days, each with its time asleep, its time to fall asleep, and the caffeine at its onset from ``CaffeineDecayRule``; the caffeine tolerance, where a hockey-stick fit finds time asleep starts to drop, from 20 to 80 mg; and the averages either side of the threshold in use. It also gives every recognised night's onset, for ``CaffeineNightRule`` (<doc:Insights>). | ``LiveSleepToleranceRepository`` |
441	| ``RestingHeartRateComparisonRule`` | The resting heart rate on the days after a caffeine night against the other days: each group's average and day count, and whether the difference is a pattern, no clear difference, or not enough days yet (<doc:Insights>) | ``ObserveRestingHeartRateComparisonUseCase``. It's one of three use cases that execute a business rule, which the owner approved on 2026-09-13 over giving one repository the other's data (see ``DayPeriodRule``). |
442	| ``StepsComparisonRule`` | The steps on the days after a caffeine night against the other days: each side's average and day count, and whether the difference is beyond twice its standard error, within it, or has too few days yet (<doc:Insights>) | ``ObserveStepsComparisonUseCase``. It's one of three use cases that execute a business rule, which the owner approved on 2026-09-13 over giving one repository the other's data (see ``DayPeriodRule``). |
443	| ``SleepPatternRule`` | The finding on the Insights tab's first card, from the Sleep screen's ``SleepCaffeineAnalysis``: the direction its comparison of time asleep shows over the threshold in use, and the confidence its smaller group allows (<doc:Insights>) | ``ObserveInsightCardUseCase``. It's another use case that executes a business rule, which the owner chose on 2026-09-13 over giving the Sleep screen's repository card 1's rule. |
444	| ``InsightNumberRule`` | Whether every number in the model's finding is one the facts gave it (<doc:Insights>) | ``LiveLanguageModelRepository``, on each insight the model writes |
445	| ``InsightFeedbackRule`` | What the "Feel right?" answers do to the first card: dismissed after "Not really", no question after an answer, both until the confidence changes, and the latest "Not really" for the next prompt (<doc:Insights>) | ``ObserveInsightCardUseCase``, with ``SleepPatternRule`` |
446	| ``CaffeineStatusRule`` | The caffeine status: the level now, the intakes still counting, when the last one is half gone, and the level at the next bedtime. It asks ``CaffeineDecayRule`` for each of them (<doc:TodayScreen>). | ``LiveCaffeineDecayRepository`` |
447	| ``DrinkLogRule`` | Whether a drink may be logged: a quantity of at least 1, consumed no later than the current time (<doc:DrinkComposer>) | ``LiveDrinkLogRepository`` |
448	| ``FavouriteDrinksRule`` | The three one-tap favourites: every drink ever logged, counted by drink and quantity together, most logged first, with starters filling the slots the log can't (<doc:OneTapLog>) | ``LiveFavouriteDrinksRepository``, and ``LiveCaffeineDecayRepository`` to find the cutoff's usual drink |
449	| ``DailyCaffeineIntakeRule`` | The caffeine in the drinks consumed on one calendar day, from midnight to midnight in a given calendar (<doc:TodayScreen>) | ``LiveDrinkLogRepository`` |
450	| ``DrinkLogDayRule`` | One calendar day of the drink log: its drinks, and its intake from ``DailyCaffeineIntakeRule`` (<doc:TodayScreen>). It also gives the last several days, oldest first (<doc:Insights>). | ``LiveDrinkLogRepository`` |
451	| ``DemoHistoryRule`` | The demo history: a fixed script of drinks for the 30 days before today, and today's up to now, each marked demo (<doc:Settings>) | ``LiveDrinkLogRepository`` |
452	| ``DayPeriodRule`` | The part of the day a moment falls in, for the greeting (<doc:TodayScreen>) | ``ObserveTimeOfDayUseCase``. Use cases that execute a business rule are exceptions the owner approved: this one on 2026-09-11, over a second clock repository, then ``ObserveRestingHeartRateComparisonUseCase`` and ``ObserveStepsComparisonUseCase`` on 2026-09-13, each over giving one repository the other's data. |
453	| ``HalfLifePriorRule`` | The starting half-life, from the factors the user reported (<doc:Onboarding>) | ``LiveUserProfileRepository``, when the factors are saved |
454	| ``SleepNightRule`` | The nights in Health's sleep intervals: sessions of sleep, held together by recorded time awake, each with at least 3 hours of sleep and some stages, with its onset, wake, deep sleep, and time awake (<doc:HalfLifeEstimator>). ``LastNightSleepRule`` shares its sessions and its union. | ``LiveHalfLifeEstimateRepository`` |
455	| ``LastNightSleepRule`` | Last night for the Apple Health card: the latest session, grouped as ``SleepNightRule`` groups them, that ends between noon yesterday and noon today with at least 3 hours of sleep, staged or not, and its time asleep. With no such session, the time in bed ending in that window (<doc:AppleHealthCard>). | ``LiveHealthDataRepository`` |

300	| `StepsDetailRobot` | ``StepsDetailView``, the screen the steps button pushes on the Insights tab | `StepsDetailViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, `title`, `verdict`, `afterCaffeine`, `otherDays`, `definition`, `chart`, `source`) |
301	| `SettingsRobot` | ``SettingsView``, the Settings tab's root list | `SettingsViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, on the scroll view, which the robot swipes, and a row per screen). Its commands open each row's screen. |
302	| `AboutYouSettingsRobot` | ``AboutYouSettingsView`` | `AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, `nameField`, `agePicker`) |
303	| `FactorsSettingsRobot` | ``FactorsSettingsView`` | `FactorsSettingsViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, an option per factor, the three trimesters) |
304	| `BedtimeSettingsRobot` | ``BedtimeSettingsView`` | `BedtimeSettingsViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, `picker`) |
305	| `PermissionSettingsRobot` | ``PermissionSettingsView`` | `PermissionSettingsViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, each permission's Allow button, status, and Settings button) |
306	| `AppLockSettingsRobot` | ``AppLockSettingsView``, around ``AppLockSettingsSection`` | `AppLockSettingsViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, `appLockOption`, `appLockError`) |
307	| `DemoHistorySettingsRobot` | ``DemoHistorySettingsView`` | `DemoHistorySettingsViewAccessibilityID` (`screen`, `addDemoButton`, `removeDemoButton`, `demoError`, `demoHealthDataOption`, `demoHealthDataError`) |
308	
309	``OneTapLogView`` appears on two screens, so its identifiers aren't unique across the app. `TodayRobot` and `DrinkComposerRobot` each look for them only inside their own screen's element, and name the favourite with the shared `OneTapFavourite` enum.
310	
311	Screens in the root tab bar run `auditAccessibilityAboveTheTabBar()` instead of `auditAccessibility()`. The Today screen scrolls under the tab bar, so at launch its lowest cards sit behind the bar, and the audit reads their contrast against the bar. The helper audits twice. At launch, it ignores contrast issues only for elements that reach under the bar or into its fade, 44 pt above the log button, and for issues the audit can't tie to an element, never for the log button itself, and fails on every other issue. Then it swipes up until the screen's first text stops moving, and audits again, so every card that was behind the bar is checked in full. At the end of a screen a little taller than the phone, the top of the content, such as a pushed Settings screen's title, sits under the navigation bar and in its fade. So the second audit ignores a contrast issue only for an element that reaches above where the content started at rest, and only if the first audit checked that element in full, because at rest it sat wholly above the bottom fade. Before each audit, it waits for a still screen: it takes screenshots until three in a row are identical, or 30 have been taken. After a launch or a scroll, the bar's glass and the scroll edge effect keep animating after the content stops, and audits taken then failed contrast on text that passes once the screen is still. Waiting for that condition, rather than for a fixed time, keeps to constitution Article II.9. The owner approved the exception and the second audit on 2026-09-12 (<doc:OneTapLog>), and chose the wait the same day, after three audits failed intermittently. On 2026-09-13, the wait grew from two identical screenshots of 20 to three of 30, after a long Today screen's second audit failed while it still moved, and the owner approved the top of the second audit (<doc:Settings>).
312	
313	## Features
314	
315	| Feature | Responsibility | Use cases | Parent |
316	|---------|----------------|-----------|--------|
317	| ``TodayFeature`` | The Today screen. It composes one child feature per card (<doc:TodayScreen>). | None of its own | ``AppFeature`` |
318	| ``DailyGreetingFeature`` | The greeting for the time of day, with the user's name and today's date | ``ObserveUserProfileUseCase``, ``ObserveTimeOfDayUseCase`` | ``TodayFeature`` |
319	| ``CaffeineDecayFeature`` | The decay card: the caffeine in your system now, the level at bedtime, when the last cup is half gone, and the curve. It refreshes the personal half-life estimate when it appears (<doc:HalfLifeEstimator>). | ``ObserveCaffeineCurveUseCase``, ``ObserveCaffeineStatusUseCase``, ``ObserveTimeOfDayUseCase``, ``RefreshHalfLifeEstimateUseCase`` | ``TodayFeature`` |
320	| ``CaffeineIntakeTodayFeature`` | The "Today" tile: the caffeine logged so far today (<doc:TodayScreen>) | ``ObserveCaffeineIntakeTodayUseCase`` | ``TodayFeature`` |
321	| ``LastCupFeature`` | The "Last cup" tile: the latest time the usual drink still leaves no more than the sleep threshold at bedtime (<doc:CaffeineCutoff>) | ``ObserveCaffeineCutoffUseCase`` | ``TodayFeature`` |
322	| ``CutoffReminderFeature`` | No screen. It schedules a local notification at each of the next seven nights' cutoffs, and schedules them again when the cutoffs or the notification permission change (<doc:CutoffReminder>) | ``ObserveUpcomingCutoffsUseCase``, ``ObservePermissionsUseCase``, ``ScheduleCutoffRemindersUseCase`` | ``AppFeature``, started by ``AppView`` |
323	| ``OneTapLogFeature`` | The one-tap row: the three favourite drinks, each logged as consumed now in one tap, with a 2-second confirmation (<doc:OneTapLog>) | ``ObserveFavouriteDrinksUseCase``, ``LogDrinkUseCase`` | ``TodayFeature`` and ``DrinkComposerFeature`` |
324	| ``DrinkLogHistoryFeature`` | The history card: one day of the drink log and its total, buttons for the day before and after, and deleting a drink once the user confirms (<doc:TodayScreen>) | ``ObserveDrinkLogDayUseCase``, ``DeleteDrinkUseCase``, ``ObserveTimeOfDayUseCase`` | ``TodayFeature`` |
325	| ``HealthSummaryFeature`` | The Apple Health card: last night's sleep, or time in bed, today's steps, and today's resting heart rate, each only when Health has it, and hidden when none does. ``TodayView`` starts its observation, because a hidden card never runs `.task` (<doc:AppleHealthCard>). | ``ObserveHealthSummaryUseCase`` | ``TodayFeature`` |

```
// Scratch probe, never merged: what XCUITest sees of a compact DatePicker, before and after it's tapped.

import XCTest

final class SpikeProbeUITests: XCTestCase {
    @MainActor
    func testProbeCompactDatePicker() throws {
        let app = XCUIApplication()
        app.launchAtOnboarding()
        try app.resolve(WelcomeRobot.self).getStarted()
        _ = try app.resolve(AboutYouRobot.self)

        let picker = app.descendants(matching: .any)["aboutYouView.agePicker"]
        XCTAssertTrue(picker.waitForExistence(timeout: 5))
        print("PROBE picker type \(picker.elementType.rawValue) label '\(picker.label)' value '\(String(describing: picker.value))'")
        print("PROBE picker tree BEGIN\n\(picker.debugDescription)\nPROBE picker tree END")

        let button = picker.buttons.firstMatch
        print("PROBE picker has button: \(button.exists) label '\(button.exists ? button.label : "")' id '\(button.exists ? button.identifier : "")'")
        (button.exists ? button : picker).tap()
        _ = app.datePickers.firstMatch.waitForExistence(timeout: 5)
        print("PROBE after tap BEGIN\n\(app.debugDescription)\nPROBE after tap END")

        let popoverPicker = app.datePickers.element(boundBy: app.datePickers.count > 1 ? 1 : 0)
        let monthYear = popoverPicker.buttons.matching(NSPredicate(format: "label CONTAINS[c] '19'")).firstMatch
        print("PROBE month-year button exists \(monthYear.exists) label '\(monthYear.exists ? monthYear.label : "")'")
        if monthYear.exists {
            monthYear.tap()
            _ = app.pickerWheels.firstMatch.waitForExistence(timeout: 5)
            print("PROBE wheels count \(app.pickerWheels.count)")
            for index in 0..<app.pickerWheels.count {
                let wheel = app.pickerWheels.element(boundBy: index)
                print("PROBE wheel \(index) value '\(String(describing: wheel.value))' id '\(wheel.identifier)'")
            }
            print("PROBE year view BEGIN\n\(app.debugDescription)\nPROBE year view END")
        }
    }
}

```

    /// Chooses the user's age on the picker.
    func chooseAge(_ age: Int, file: StaticString = #filePath, line: UInt = #line) {
        require(agePicker, "The age picker", file: file, line: line)
        let wheel = agePicker.elementType == .pickerWheel ? agePicker : agePicker.pickerWheels.firstMatch
        wheel.adjust(toPickerWheelValue: "\(age)")
    }
```

    /// Chooses "Prefer not to say" for the date of birth.
    func preferNotToGiveDateOfBirth(file: StaticString = #filePath, line: UInt = #line) {
        require(preferNotToSayOption, "The \"Prefer not to say\" option", file: file, line: line)
        preferNotToSayOption.tap()
    }

    /// Checks that the date-of-birth picker shows, and whether "Prefer not to say" is chosen.
    func verifyPrefersNotToGiveDateOfBirth(_ isChosen: Bool, file: StaticString = #filePath, line: UInt = #line) {
        require(birthDatePicker, "The date-of-birth picker", file: file, line: line)
        require(preferNotToSayOption, "The \"Prefer not to say\" option", file: file, line: line)
        let selected = NSPredicate(format: "isSelected == %@", NSNumber(value: isChosen))
        let result = XCTWaiter().wait(
            for: [XCTNSPredicateExpectation(predicate: selected, object: preferNotToSayOption)], timeout: 5)
        XCTAssertEqual(
            result, .completed, "\"Prefer not to say\" isn't \(isChosen ? "chosen" : "unchosen").", file: file,
            line: line)
    }

- `new_string`:

```
/// Drives `AboutYouSettingsView`, Settings' About you screen: the name and the date of birth.
struct AboutYouSettingsRobot: Robot {
    /// The screen's identifier.
    static let screenIdentifier = AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID.screen

    /// The app the robot drives.
    let app: XCUIApplication

    private var screen: XCUIElement { app.descendants(matching: .any)[AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID.screen] }
    /// The name field wraps, so it may be a text view rather than a text field.
    private var nameField: XCUIElement {
        app.descendants(matching: .any)[AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID.nameField]
    }
    private var birthDatePicker: XCUIElement {
        app.descendants(matching: .any)[AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID.birthDatePicker]
    }
    private var preferNotToSayOption: XCUIElement {
        app.buttons[AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID.preferNotToSayOption]
    }

    /// Chooses "Prefer not to say" for the date of birth, which saves at once.
    func preferNotToGiveDateOfBirth(file: StaticString = #filePath, line: UInt = #line) {
        reveal(preferNotToSayOption, on: screen, "The \"Prefer not to say\" option", file: file, line: line)
        preferNotToSayOption.tap()
    }

    /// Checks that the date-of-birth picker shows, and whether "Prefer not to say" is chosen.
    func verifyPrefersNotToGiveDateOfBirth(_ isChosen: Bool, file: StaticString = #filePath, line: UInt = #line) {
        reveal(birthDatePicker, on: screen, "The date-of-birth picker", file: file, line: line)
        reveal(preferNotToSayOption, on: screen, "The \"Prefer not to say\" option", file: file, line: line)
        let selected = NSPredicate(format: "isSelected == %@", NSNumber(value: isChosen))
        let result = XCTWaiter().wait(
            for: [XCTNSPredicateExpectation(predicate: selected, object: preferNotToSayOption)], timeout: 5)
        XCTAssertEqual(
            result, .completed, "\"Prefer not to say\" isn't \(isChosen ? "chosen" : "unchosen").", file: file,
            line: line)
    }
```

        let store = TestStore(initialState: ProfileSettingsFeature.State()) {
            ProfileSettingsFeature()
        } withDependencies: {
            $0.calendar = Self.utc
            $0.observeUserProfile = ObserveUserProfileUseCase(repository: FakeUserProfileRepository(profiles: []))
            $0.observeTimeOfDay = ObserveTimeOfDayUseCase(currentTime: FakeCurrentTimeRepository(date: Self.now))
        }

        await store.send(.task)
        await store.receive(\.timeOfDayUpdated) {
            $0.birthDatePicker = Self.picker
        }
        await store.finish()
    }

    @Test func theDateOfBirthFillsInOnceThePickerIsKnownAndEachProfileRefillsTheFields() async {
        let store = Self.store(ProfileSettingsFeature.State())
        let alex = UserProfile(name: "Alex", dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 1990, month: 3, day: 3))
        let sam = UserProfile(
            name: "Sam", dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 1980, month: 1, day: 1), bedtime: Self.elevenPM)

        await store.send(.profileUpdated(alex)) {
            $0.savedProfile = alex
            $0.name = "Alex"
        }
        await store.send(.timeOfDayUpdated(Self.timeOfDay)) {
            $0.birthDatePicker = Self.picker
            $0.birthDate = Self.day(1990, 3, 3)
        }
        await store.send(.profileUpdated(sam)) {
            $0.savedProfile = sam
            $0.name = "Sam"
            $0.birthDate = Self.day(1980, 1, 1)
            $0.bedtime = Self.elevenPM
        }
    }

    @Test func aSavedDateOfBirthOutsideThePickerIsLeftEmpty() async {
        let store = Self.store(
            ProfileSettingsFeature.State(birthDate: Self.day(1996, 3, 3), birthDatePicker: Self.picker))
        let child = UserProfile(dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 2020, month: 1, day: 1))

        await store.send(.profileUpdated(child)) {
            $0.savedProfile = child
            $0.birthDate = nil
        }
    }

//  _______  __   __  ___   ___      ___      _______  __    _  _______
// |       ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |   _   ||  |  | ||       |
// |   _   ||  | |  ||   | |   |    |   |    |  |_|  ||   |_| ||   _   |

// |____||_||_______||___| |_______||_______||__| |__||_|  |__||____||_|
//
// Half-LifeTests ProfileSettingsFeatureDateOfBirthTests
//

import ComposableArchitecture
import Foundation
import Testing

@testable import Half_Life

/// Checks the date of birth on Settings' About you screen, against SETPROF-1, SETPROF-3, and SETPROF-5 in the Settings
/// article. It's an extension of ``ProfileSettingsFeatureTests``, which keeps that suite within SwiftLint's body length.
extension ProfileSettingsFeatureTests {

    // MARK: - SETPROF-1: the date of birth fills in once the picker's dates are known

    @Test func taskReducesThePickersDatesIntoState() async {
        let store = TestStore(initialState: ProfileSettingsFeature.State()) {
            ProfileSettingsFeature()
        } withDependencies: {
            $0.calendar = Self.utc
            $0.observeUserProfile = ObserveUserProfileUseCase(repository: FakeUserProfileRepository(profiles: []))
            $0.observeTimeOfDay = ObserveTimeOfDayUseCase(currentTime: FakeCurrentTimeRepository(date: Self.now))
        }

        await store.send(.task)
        await store.receive(\.timeOfDayUpdated) {
            $0.birthDatePicker = Self.picker
        }
        await store.finish()
    }

    @Test func theDateOfBirthFillsInOnceThePickerIsKnownAndEachProfileRefillsTheFields() async {
        let store = Self.store(ProfileSettingsFeature.State())
        let alex = UserProfile(name: "Alex", dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 1990, month: 3, day: 3))
        let sam = UserProfile(
            name: "Sam", dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 1980, month: 1, day: 1), bedtime: Self.elevenPM)

        await store.send(.profileUpdated(alex)) {
            $0.savedProfile = alex
            $0.name = "Alex"
        }
        await store.send(.timeOfDayUpdated(Self.timeOfDay)) {
            $0.birthDatePicker = Self.picker
            $0.birthDate = Self.day(1990, 3, 3)
        }
        await store.send(.profileUpdated(sam)) {
            $0.savedProfile = sam
            $0.name = "Sam"
            $0.birthDate = Self.day(1980, 1, 1)
            $0.bedtime = Self.elevenPM
        }
    }

    @Test func aSavedDateOfBirthOutsideThePickerIsLeftEmpty() async {
        let store = Self.store(
            ProfileSettingsFeature.State(birthDate: Self.day(1996, 3, 3), birthDatePicker: Self.picker))
        let child = UserProfile(dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 2020, month: 1, day: 1))

        await store.send(.profileUpdated(child)) {
            $0.savedProfile = child
            $0.birthDate = nil
        }
    }

    // MARK: - SETPROF-3: a date of birth, or "Prefer not to say", saves as soon as it's chosen

    @Test func choosingADateSavesItWithTheName() async {
        let repository = FakeUserProfileRepository()
        let store = Self.store(
            ProfileSettingsFeature.State(name: "Alex", isEditingName: true), repository: repository)

        await store.send(.birthDateChosen(Self.day(1986, 3, 3))) {
            $0.birthDate = Self.day(1986, 3, 3)
            $0.isEditingName = false
            $0.savesInFlight = 1
        }
        await store.receive(\.saveFinished) {
            $0.savesInFlight = 0
        }

        #expect(
            await repository.savedAboutYou == [
                .init(name: "Alex", dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 1986, month: 3, day: 3))
            ])
    }

    @Test func preferringNotToSayClearsTheDateAndSavesIt() async {
        let repository = FakeUserProfileRepository()
        let store = Self.store(
            ProfileSettingsFeature.State(name: "Alex", birthDate: Self.day(1986, 3, 3)), repository: repository)

        await store.send(.preferNotToSayTapped) {
            $0.birthDate = nil
            $0.savesInFlight = 1
        }
        await store.receive(\.saveFinished) {
            $0.savesInFlight = 0
        }

        #expect(await repository.savedAboutYou == [.init(name: "Alex", dateOfBirth: nil)])
    }

    @Test func preferringNotToSayWithNoDateSavesNothing() async {
        let repository = FakeUserProfileRepository()
        let store = Self.store(ProfileSettingsFeature.State(name: "Alex"), repository: repository)

        await store.send(.preferNotToSayTapped)

        #expect(await repository.savedAboutYou.isEmpty)
    }

    // MARK: - SETPROF-5: a failed save puts the saved date of birth back

    @Test func aFailedDateSavePutsTheSavedDateBack() async {
        let saved = UserProfile(name: "Alex", dateOfBirth: DateOfBirth(year: 1990, month: 3, day: 3))
        let store = Self.store(
            ProfileSettingsFeature.State(
                savedProfile: saved, name: "Alex", birthDate: Self.day(1990, 3, 3), birthDatePicker: Self.picker),
            repository: FakeUserProfileRepository(error: SaveFailed()))

        await store.send(.birthDateChosen(Self.day(1986, 3, 3))) {
            $0.birthDate = Self.day(1986, 3, 3)
            $0.savesInFlight = 1
        }
        await store.receive(\.saveFinished) {
            $0.savesInFlight = 0
            $0.birthDate = Self.day(1990, 3, 3)
        }
    }
}

```

````
7	## Rules
8	
9	1. **Scope: the entire project.** Log every interaction with an AI tool about Half-Life, whether or not it changes a file. That includes:
10	   - generating code, tests, documentation, or configuration;
11	   - code review and debugging;
12	   - planning and design discussions;
13	   - questions and explanations.
14	
15	   This applies to every contributor and every AI tool: coding agents, chat assistants, and IDE assistants.
16	2. **One entry per task.** A task is one request to an AI tool plus the follow-up interaction needed to complete it. A session with several unrelated requests gets several entries. Inline code-completion tools (e.g. Xcode predictive code completion) are logged once per working session in which they were used, not once per suggestion.
17	3. **Timestamps.**
18	   1. Every entry records when its task started and ended.
19	   2. Its **Interactions** timeline gives the time of each human prompt or decision.
20	   3. Timestamps are local time with UTC offset in the form `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm`, exactly what `date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M %z'` prints. Within the Interactions timeline, `HH:MM` is enough while the date is unchanged.
21	   4. Take timestamps from the clock at the time, never from memory afterwards. If a time has to be reconstructed, prefer the session transcript's timestamps (Rule 9). Otherwise, prefix the time with `~` and say what it was reconstructed from.
22	4. **Chronological and append-only.** Append entries at the bottom of the Log section, in order of start time. An entry may be revised while its task's changes are uncommitted. Once committed, it is never edited or deleted. To correct a committed entry, add a new entry that references it.
23	5. **When to log.** Write the entry before the task is reported as finished. If the task changed files, commit the entry together with those changes. Otherwise commit it on its own.
24	6. **Honesty.** Record what the AI actually did, including incorrect answers, rejected suggestions, withdrawn work, and outputs that needed significant human correction.
25	7. **No sensitive data.** Never paste secrets, credentials, personal data, health data, or customer data into an entry or a transcript (Rule 9). Summarize prompts instead of copying them verbatim when they contain such data.
26	8. **Accountability.** Every entry names the human responsible for the interaction and for reviewing and accepting its outcome.
27	9. **Transcripts.** The full transcript of every AI session is kept in `ai_transcripts/`, one file per session.
28	   1. Claude Code sessions are exported with `/usr/bin/python3 scripts/export_transcripts.py`, which renders Claude Code's local session files as Markdown. Re-run it before every commit so each committed transcript covers its session up to that point. Transcripts are generated files: never edit one by hand. A committed transcript changes only by being re-exported.
29	   2. A transcript includes every human prompt, reply, and tool call in full, each with its time. Tool results are cut to their first 40 lines. Images, system context, and harness metadata are left out. Claude Code doesn't store the AI's reasoning or the progress updates it writes between tool calls, so transcripts can't include them.
30	   3. The exporter redacts email addresses and the home directory path. It can't recognize secrets or health data typed into a prompt or read from a file, so review the transcript diff before committing, and apply Rule 7 to it.
31	   4. For other AI tools, save the tool's own export of the conversation in `ai_transcripts/` if it offers one. Otherwise, say in the entry's **Transcript** field that no transcript exists.
32	   5. Every entry names the transcript of the session it happened in. One session can cover several entries, and an entry whose task spans sessions lists each transcript.
33	
34	### Entry format
35	
36	```markdown
37	### YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm — <short title>
38	
39	- **Started:** YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm
40	- **Ended:** YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM ±hhmm
41	- **Human:** <name of the responsible person>
42	- **AI tool / model:** <e.g. Claude Code / Claude Opus 5>
43	- **Transcript:** <path(s) under `ai_transcripts/`, or "None" and why>
44	- **Type:** <one or more of: change, question, review, debugging, planning>
45	- **Request:** <one or two sentences summarizing what was asked>
46	- **Interactions:**
````

### 16:10:01 · Claude

The red step is now confirmed. Against the untouched snapshot, my new tests fail to compile for the expected reasons: `DateOfBirth` and `BirthDatePicker` don't exist, the reducers have no `birthDate`, `birthDatePicker`, or `birthDateChosen`, and `SaveAboutYouUseCase` still demands a clock.

Nothing else can start until two background runs report back, and both will wake me when they finish:
- **The accessibility-audit spike.** A compact date picker on both About you screens, run through their audits. iOS's wheel date picker failed this app's Dynamic Type check before, so the result decides whether the design stands.
- **The unit suite** in my isolated copy.

**Done so far, in an isolated copy (nothing in the main tree yet):**
- **Domain:** a new `DateOfBirth` type, a real Gregorian day with no time zone, so it doesn't shift when you travel. `UserProfile` stores it in place of `birthYear`. The sleep-range rule computes the exact age, and the save use case no longer needs a clock or a calendar.
- **Data:** the profile file moves to version 2. A version-1 file's birth year is dropped rather than turned into an invented date. **That's my call, not yours; say if you'd rather convert it.**
- **Both screens:** a shared compact date picker on a card, with a "Prefer not to say" option under it. It offers ages 13 to 100 and shows a 30-year-old's date until you pick one. One quirk: you can't pick that exact suggested date directly, because re-tapping the date already shown doesn't register a change. Picking another date and then going back works.
- **Tests, docs, catalog:** reducer, use case, repository, and file tests are written. The Onboarding, Settings, and Architecture articles are updated, and "Date of birth" is in the catalog. swift-format and SwiftLint are clean on every changed file.

**Still to do:**
- An XCUITest probe of whether a robot can pick a date using identifiers only. UI-ONB-2 currently picks an age, so that step has to be replaced one way or another.
- Merge back into the main tree. half-life-fa and half-life-bf are editing the same Settings reducer, catalog, and docs.
- The full unit and UI test run with coverage, the doc build, and the `ai_log.md` entry.

**16:16:44 · Tool call: Bash** — Resolve the merge conflicts and check UI-SET numbering

```sh
cd /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-adamure-Development-Femmli-Half-Life/e929ff11-d161-4d77-a7dd-9ff08c011566/scratchpad/dryrun && mkdir -p resolved && python3 - <<'EOF'
import re
def blocks(text):
    pat = re.compile(r'^<<<<<<< [^\n]*\n(.*?)^=======\n(.*?)^>>>>>>> [^\n]*\n', re.S | re.M)
    return pat
def resolve(name, rules):
    t = open(name).read()
    pat = blocks(t)
    i = 0
    def sub(m):
        nonlocal i
        ours = m.group(1).splitlines(keepends=True); theirs = m.group(2).splitlines(keepends=True)
        out = rules[i](ours, theirs); i += 1
        return ''.join(out)
    t2 = pat.sub(sub, t)
    assert i == len(rules), (name, i, len(rules))
    assert '<<<<<<<' not in t2 and '>>>>>>>' not in t2
    return t2
ob = resolve('Onboarding.md', [lambda o, t: [t[0], o[1]]])
def uiset(o, t):
    mine = o[0].replace('| UI-SET-7 |', '| UI-SET-8 |')
    return [t[0], mine]
st = resolve('Settings.md', [lambda o, t: [t[0]] + o, uiset])
ar = resolve('Architecture.md', [lambda o, t: [t[0], o[1]], lambda o, t: [t[0], o[0]]])
open('resolved/Onboarding.md','w').write(ob); open('resolved/Settings.md','w').write(st); open('resolved/Architecture.md','w').write(ar)
ui = open('SettingsUITests.swift').read()
print([l for l in ui.splitlines() if 'UI-SET-' in l])
EOF
grep -n "UI-SET-7\|UI-SET-8" resolved/Settings.md | cut -c1-200; grep -c "birthYear\|agePicker" resolved/*.md
```

- **The summary's buttons sit on the solid page, and onboarding's primary button has no shadow.** The audit failed "Take me to Today" for contrast while the summary could scroll under it, and again while the shadow of "Log my first cup" fell across it. The drink composer's Add button has no shadow either.
- **The name field wraps**, up to three lines. As a one-line field, the audit reported that its text could be clipped at larger Dynamic Type sizes.
- **The bedtime is the system's compact time picker**: a button that shows the time in the locale's clock ("10:30 PM" or "22:30") and opens iOS's own wheel. Its label, "Bedtime", sits beside it on the card. The system picker's inline wheel failed the audit's Dynamic Type check on 2026-09-12 ("Dynamic Type font sizes are unsupported"), so the bedtime was first two wheels built from SwiftUI pickers. The compact style passes the audit on a still screen (see "What changed while building"). The age wheel, a SwiftUI picker, passes too.
- A selected chip or factor has the selected trait, so VoiceOver doesn't depend on its fill.
- Every step scrolls vertically, so nothing is lost at the largest Dynamic Type sizes (Article VI.2). **No screen scrolls sideways**: its content fits the screen's width, and UI-ONB-8 checks each screen with a sideways swipe, at the default and the largest text size. iOS 26's back gesture still works from anywhere on a pushed step, and slides the page sideways as it goes back. The owner chose on 2026-09-12 to keep it.
```

```sh
cd /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-adamure-Development-Femmli-Half-Life/e929ff11-d161-4d77-a7dd-9ff08c011566/scratchpad && cp OnboardingFormatTests.work.swift.aside work/Half-LifeTests/Presentation/OnboardingFormatTests.swift && python3 - <<'EOF'
import filecmp, os, shutil, subprocess
M = '~/Development/Femmli/Half-Life'
skip = {'build', '.git', 'ai_transcripts', 'xcuserdata', '.DS_Store'}
def walk(root):
    out = set()
    for d, dirs, files in os.walk(root):
        dirs[:] = [x for x in dirs if x not in skip]
        for f in files:
            if f in skip: continue
            out.add(os.path.relpath(os.path.join(d, f), root))
    return out
base, main = walk('base'), walk(M)
report = []
for rel in sorted(base | main):
    b, m, w = os.path.join('base', rel), os.path.join(M, rel), os.path.join('work', rel)
    inb, inm, inw = os.path.exists(b), os.path.exists(m), os.path.exists(w)
    if inb and inm and filecmp.cmp(b, m, shallow=False): continue
    if not inb and inm:
        if inw and not filecmp.cmp(w, m, shallow=False): report.append(f"CLASH new in both: {rel}"); continue
        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(w), exist_ok=True); os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(b), exist_ok=True)
        shutil.copy2(m, w); shutil.copy2(m, b); report.append(f"added {rel}"); continue
    if inb and not inm:
        if inw and filecmp.cmp(w, b, shallow=False): os.remove(w); os.remove(b); report.append(f"deleted {rel}")
        else: report.append(f"CLASH deleted in main but changed in work: {rel}")
        continue
    if inw and filecmp.cmp(w, b, shallow=False):
        shutil.copy2(m, w); shutil.copy2(m, b); report.append(f"copied {rel}")
    else:
        r = subprocess.run(['git', 'merge-file', '-p', w, b, m], capture_output=True)
        open(w, 'wb').write(r.stdout); shutil.copy2(m, b)
        report.append(f"merged {rel} (conflicts: {r.returncode})")
print('\n'.join(report))
EOF

import re
pat = re.compile(r'^<<<<<<< [^\n]*\n(.*?)^=======\n(.*?)^>>>>>>> [^\n]*\n', re.S | re.M)
def resolve(name, rules):
    t = open(name).read(); i = 0
    def sub(m):
        nonlocal i
        o = m.group(1).splitlines(keepends=True); th = m.group(2).splitlines(keepends=True)
        out = rules[i](o, th); i += 1; return ''.join(out)
    t = pat.sub(sub, t)
    assert i == len(rules), (name, i, len(rules)); assert '<<<<<<<' not in t and '>>>>>>>' not in t
    open(name, 'w').write(t); print("resolved", name, i)
D = 'Half-Life/Documentation.docc/'
resolve(D + 'Onboarding.md', [lambda o, t: [t[0], o[1]]])
resolve(D + 'Settings.md', [lambda o, t: [t[0]] + o, lambda o, t: [t[0], o[0].replace('| UI-SET-7 |', '| UI-SET-8 |')]])
resolve(D + 'Architecture.md', [lambda o, t: [t[0], o[1]], lambda o, t: [t[0], o[0]]])
p = 'Half-LifeUITests/SettingsUITests.swift'; ui = open(p).read()
hdr = '/// Drives the Settings tab against UI-SET-1 to UI-SET-7 in the Settings article.'
mine = '    /// UI-SET-7: About you shows the date-of-birth picker'
assert ui.count(hdr) == 1 and ui.count(mine) == 1
open(p, 'w').write(ui.replace(hdr, hdr.replace('7', '8')).replace(mine, mine.replace('UI-SET-7', 'UI-SET-8')))
print("renumbered", p)
EOF

- `content`:

```
### 2026-09-13 15:25 -0400 — Replace the age wheel with a date-of-birth picker

- **Started:** 2026-09-13 15:25 -0400
- **Ended:** TBD
- **Human:** Adam Ure
- **AI tool / model:** Claude Code / Claude Opus 5
- **Transcript:** `ai_transcripts/2026-09-13-1525-e929ff11.md`
- **Type:** change, planning
- **Request:** Replace the age scroll wheel with a date picker, in onboarding's About you step and Settings' About you screen, for a native experience and a simpler implementation.
- **Interactions:**
  - `15:25` Asked for the change.
  - `~15:31` Answered two questions (the time is from the first command run after the answers). Store the full date of birth, which the AI recommended, over keeping the birth year. Show a "Prefer not to say" option under the picker, over the AI's recommended "Add date of birth" button that reveals it.
- **AI contribution:**
  - **Research before asking.** Found that iOS's wheel date picker had failed the audit's Dynamic Type check on 2026-09-12, which is why the age and bedtime were SwiftUI wheels, and raised it as a risk.
  - **Isolation.** Worked in scratch copies of the tree, rebased twice onto the peers' changes (3-way, with conflicts resolved by keeping both sides), so red-phase compile errors never reached the other sessions. Told the peers which shared files it would touch.
  - **Red, confirmed after the fact.** The tests were written first, but the red run came after the production code, because the simulator was busy with the spike. It was then run against an untouched snapshot: the tests failed to compile on the missing `DateOfBirth`, `BirthDatePicker`, `birthDate`, `birthDateChosen`, and the use case's removed clock.
  - **Domain:** `DateOfBirth`, a real Gregorian day with no time zone, with the day a moment falls on, the start of its day, and the age at a moment (DOB-1 to DOB-3). `UserProfile.birthYear` became `dateOfBirth`. `SleepNeedRule` counts whole years since the date of birth (SLEEPNEED-2 and SLEEPNEED-3 reworded). `SaveAboutYouUseCase` saves the date of birth as given, with no clock or calendar.
  - **Data:** the profile file is version 2, storing the date of birth as year, month, and day. A version 1 file's birth year reads as no date of birth, and a stored day that isn't real throws (PROFFILE-5). This was the AI's choice, not asked.
  - **Presentation:** `BirthDatePicker` (ages 13 to 100 from the start of today, a 30-year-old's date shown until one is picked, and a saved date filled in only if offered) and `BirthDateField` (the compact `DatePicker` on a card and a "Prefer not to say" `SettingsOption`), shared by `AboutYouFeature` and `ProfileSettingsFeature`, each with `birthDateChosen` and `preferNotToSayTapped` (ONB-10, SETPROF-1 to SETPROF-5).
  - **Tests changed, with the reason:** `SleepNeedRuleTests`, `ProfileSaveUseCaseTests` (the calendar-year test went, because the use case no longer counts years), the reducer tests, and one `OnboardingDependencyTests` assertion that the save use case held the clock, which it no longer has. Seven Settings date tests moved to `ProfileSettingsFeatureDateOfBirthTests`, an extension that keeps the suite within SwiftLint's body length.
  - **UI tests:** robots' "Prefer not to say" commands, UI-ONB-10 and UI-SET-8. A peer had taken UI-SET-7. TBD: date picking and UI-ONB-2.
  - **Docs:** Onboarding (decision note, `DateOfBirth`, requirements), Settings (decision row, SETPROF, UI-SET-8), Architecture (robots, features, use case, repository, stored data). One catalog key, "Date of birth", and reworded comments.
- **Human changes:** The two decisions above.
- **Files:** TBD
- **Verification:** TBD
- **Notes:** A date equal to the suggested one can't be picked directly, because the picker reports no change; picking another date first works. TBD.

```

- **Interactions:**
  - `15:25` Asked for the change.
  - `~15:31` Answered two questions (reconstructed from the first command run after the answers). Store the full date of birth, which the AI recommended, over keeping the birth year. Show a "Prefer not to say" option under the picker, over an "Add date of birth" button that reveals it, which the AI recommended.
  - `17:16` Asked the AI to wrap up this entry. The work stopped there, unmerged (see "Notes").
- **AI contribution:**
  - **Research before asking.** Found that iOS's wheel date picker failed the audit's Dynamic Type check on 2026-09-12, which is why the age and bedtime were SwiftUI wheels, and raised it as a risk. Another session has since moved the bedtime to the compact time picker, which passes the audit on a still screen.
  - **Isolation.** Worked in scratch copies of the tree, never in the main tree, and rebased twice onto the peers' changes (3-way, every conflict resolved by keeping both sides). Told the peer sessions which shared files it would touch. A peer's app-version work had taken UI-SET-7, so this change's Settings UI test is UI-SET-8.
  - **Red, confirmed late.** The tests were written first, but the red run came after the production code, because the simulator was busy. It was then run against an untouched snapshot, where the tests failed to compile on the missing `DateOfBirth`, `BirthDatePicker`, `birthDate`, and `birthDateChosen`, and on the use case's removed clock.
  - **Domain.** `DateOfBirth`, a real Gregorian day with no time zone: the day a moment falls on, the start of its day, and the age at a moment (DOB-1 to DOB-3). `UserProfile.birthYear` became `dateOfBirth`. `SleepNeedRule` counts whole years since the date of birth. `SaveAboutYouUseCase` saves the date of birth as given, with no clock or calendar.
  - **Data.** The profile file is version 2, storing the date of birth as year, month, and day. A version 1 file's birth year reads as no date of birth, and a stored day that isn't real throws (PROFFILE-5). Dropping the old birth year was the AI's choice, not asked.
  - **Presentation.** `BirthDatePicker` (ages 13 to 100 from the start of today, a 30-year-old's date shown until one is picked, a saved date filled in only if offered) and `BirthDateField` (iOS's compact `DatePicker` on a card, with a "Prefer not to say" `SettingsOption`), shared by `AboutYouFeature` and `ProfileSettingsFeature`, each with `birthDateChosen` and `preferNotToSayTapped` (ONB-10, SETPROF-1 to SETPROF-5).
  - **Tests changed, with the reason.** `SleepNeedRuleTests`, `ProfileSaveUseCaseTests` (its calendar-year test went, because the use case no longer counts years), both reducers' tests, and one `OnboardingDependencyTests` assertion that the save use case held the clock, which it no longer has. Seven Settings date tests moved to `ProfileSettingsFeatureDateOfBirthTests`, an extension that keeps the suite within SwiftLint's body length.
  - **UI tests.** The robots gained "Prefer not to say" commands and lost `chooseAge(_:)`. Added UI-ONB-10 and UI-SET-8.
  - **Docs and catalog.** Onboarding (the decision, `DateOfBirth`, and the requirements), Settings (a decision row, SETPROF, UI-SET-8), and Architecture (robots, features, use case, repository, stored data). One catalog key, "Date of birth", and reworded comments on "Age" and "Prefer not to say".
- **Human changes:** The two decisions above.
- **Files:** Only `ai_log.md` changed in the main tree. The change itself is in the scratch copy `scratchpad/work` of this session, not merged:
  - Added: `Domain/Entities/DateOfBirth.swift`, `Features/Onboarding/BirthDatePicker.swift`, `Features/Onboarding/BirthDateField.swift`, `Half-LifeTests/Domain/DateOfBirthTests.swift`, `Half-LifeTests/Presentation/ProfileSettingsFeatureDateOfBirthTests.swift`.
  - Modified: `UserProfile`, `UserProfileRepository`, `SaveAboutYouUseCase`, `ObserveRecommendedSleepUseCase`, `SleepNeedRule`, `FileProfileDataSource`, `LiveUserProfileRepository`, `UserProfileDependencies`, `AboutYouFeature`, `AboutYouView`, `AboutYouViewAccessibilityID`, `ProfileSettingsFeature`, `AboutYouSettingsView`, `AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID`, `Localizable.xcstrings`, the Onboarding, Settings, and Architecture articles, nine unit test files and the fake repository, `AboutYouRobot`, `AboutYouSettingsRobot`, `OnboardingUITests`, and `SettingsUITests`.

- `docbuild` builds docs for every project target, including `Half-LifeWidgets`; check each target's `*-diagnostics.json`, not only the app's.
- With a dozen sessions building at once (load average ~900 on 2026-09-13), simulator boots outlast `xcodebuild`'s 60 s wait ("Timed out trying to boot simulator"), so UI tests fail before running. Boot and `simctl bootstatus -b` first, run `test-without-building`, and avoid stacking several of your own builds at once.
- Peers' red-phase tests often break the shared test target. In a scratch copy, set aside only the non-yours files that fail to compile, never your own, and say which in the log.
```

### 2026-09-13 15:25 -0400 — Replace the age wheel with a date-of-birth picker

- **Started:** 2026-09-13 15:25 -0400
- **Ended:** 2026-09-13 17:16 -0400
- **Human:** Adam Ure
- **AI tool / model:** Claude Code / Claude Opus 5
- **Transcript:** `ai_transcripts/2026-09-13-1525-e929ff11.md`
- **Type:** change, planning
- **Request:** Replace the age scroll wheel with a date picker, in onboarding's About you step and Settings' About you screen, for a native experience and a simpler implementation.
- **Interactions:**
  - `15:25` Asked for the change.
  - `~15:31` Answered two questions (reconstructed from the first command run after the answers). Store the full date of birth, which the AI recommended, over keeping the birth year. Show a "Prefer not to say" option under the picker, over an "Add date of birth" button that reveals it, which the AI recommended.
  - `17:16` Asked the AI to wrap up this entry. The work stopped there, unmerged (see "Notes").
- **AI contribution:**
  - **Research before asking.** Found that iOS's wheel date picker failed the audit's Dynamic Type check on 2026-09-12, which is why the age and bedtime were SwiftUI wheels, and raised it as a risk. Another session has since moved the bedtime to the compact time picker, which passes the audit on a still screen.
  - **Isolation.** Worked in scratch copies of the tree, never in the main tree, and rebased twice onto the peers' changes (3-way, every conflict resolved by keeping both sides). Told the peer sessions which shared files it would touch. A peer's app-version work had taken UI-SET-7, so this change's Settings UI test is UI-SET-8.
  - **Red, confirmed late.** The tests were written first, but the red run came after the production code, because the simulator was busy. It was then run against an untouched snapshot, where the tests failed to compile on the missing `DateOfBirth`, `BirthDatePicker`, `birthDate`, and `birthDateChosen`, and on the use case's removed clock.
  - **Domain.** `DateOfBirth`, a real Gregorian day with no time zone: the day a moment falls on, the start of its day, and the age at a moment (DOB-1 to DOB-3). `UserProfile.birthYear` became `dateOfBirth`. `SleepNeedRule` counts whole years since the date of birth. `SaveAboutYouUseCase` saves the date of birth as given, with no clock or calendar.
  - **Data.** The profile file is version 2, storing the date of birth as year, month, and day. A version 1 file's birth year reads as no date of birth, and a stored day that isn't real throws (PROFFILE-5). Dropping the old birth year was the AI's choice, not asked.
  - **Presentation.** `BirthDatePicker` (ages 13 to 100 from the start of today, a 30-year-old's date shown until one is picked, a saved date filled in only if offered) and `BirthDateField` (iOS's compact `DatePicker` on a card, with a "Prefer not to say" `SettingsOption`), shared by `AboutYouFeature` and `ProfileSettingsFeature`, each with `birthDateChosen` and `preferNotToSayTapped` (ONB-10, SETPROF-1 to SETPROF-5).
  - **Tests changed, with the reason.** `SleepNeedRuleTests`, `ProfileSaveUseCaseTests` (its calendar-year test went, because the use case no longer counts years), both reducers' tests, and one `OnboardingDependencyTests` assertion that the save use case held the clock, which it no longer has. Seven Settings date tests moved to `ProfileSettingsFeatureDateOfBirthTests`, an extension that keeps the suite within SwiftLint's body length.
  - **UI tests.** The robots gained "Prefer not to say" commands and lost `chooseAge(_:)`. Added UI-ONB-10 and UI-SET-8.
  - **Docs and catalog.** Onboarding (the decision, `DateOfBirth`, and the requirements), Settings (a decision row, SETPROF, UI-SET-8), and Architecture (robots, features, use case, repository, stored data). One catalog key, "Date of birth", and reworded comments on "Age" and "Prefer not to say".
  - **The log entry itself.** First appended at 17:16. Another session's rewrite of `ai_log.md` then dropped it, so it was appended again.
- **Human changes:** The two decisions above.
- **Files:** Only `ai_log.md` changed in the main tree. The change itself is in the scratch copy `scratchpad/work` of this session, not merged:
  - Added: `Domain/Entities/DateOfBirth.swift`, `Features/Onboarding/BirthDatePicker.swift`, `Features/Onboarding/BirthDateField.swift`, `Half-LifeTests/Domain/DateOfBirthTests.swift`, `Half-LifeTests/Presentation/ProfileSettingsFeatureDateOfBirthTests.swift`.
  - Modified: `UserProfile`, `UserProfileRepository`, `SaveAboutYouUseCase`, `ObserveRecommendedSleepUseCase`, `SleepNeedRule`, `FileProfileDataSource`, `LiveUserProfileRepository`, `UserProfileDependencies`, `AboutYouFeature`, `AboutYouView`, `AboutYouViewAccessibilityID`, `ProfileSettingsFeature`, `AboutYouSettingsView`, `AboutYouSettingsViewAccessibilityID`, `Localizable.xcstrings`, the Onboarding, Settings, and Architecture articles, nine unit test files and the fake repository, `AboutYouRobot`, `AboutYouSettingsRobot`, `OnboardingUITests`, and `SettingsUITests`.
- **Verification:**
  - Unit tests, in the scratch copy: 1,303 tests in 241 suites passed, with 107 known issues, all expected ones. Every suite this change touched passed. Four other sessions' unfinished test files were set aside for the run, because they didn't compile: `CaffeineDecayFeatureTests`, `LanguageModelDataSourceTests`, `SimulatedLanguageModelDataSourceTests`, and `InsightLanguageModelTests`.
  - swift-format lint `--strict` and SwiftLint `--strict`: clean on every file this change touched.
  - Not run: the UI tests, the accessibility audits, coverage, and `docbuild`. The audit spike never ran a test. The machine's load average reached about 900, and each attempt timed out booting its simulator.
- **Notes:**
  - **Not merged and not committed.** Before it can land: the UI test target doesn't compile, because UI-ONB-2 still calls the removed `chooseAge(_:)`. A robot command for picking a date is undesigned, pending a probe of whether the compact picker's popover can be driven by identifier (constitution Article II.6). The compact date picker's accessibility audit is unproven, and the Onboarding and Settings articles' accessibility bullets still describe the age wheel. Then a 3-way merge into the main tree, the full unit and UI run with coverage (the 80% check), and `docbuild`.
  - A date equal to the suggested one can't be picked directly, because the picker reports no change. Picking another date first works.
  - Open for the owner: whether a version 1 file's birth year should be dropped, as built, or converted.

### 17:19:53 · Claude

I've written this task's entry at the end of `ai_log.md`. The date-of-birth change itself is still not in the main tree: it's finished in my scratch copy, but a UI test still calls the old age picker and none of the UI tests, audits, coverage or doc build have run. Nothing is committed.

**Another session is overwriting `ai_log.md`.** I appended my entry at 17:16. By 17:18 the file had been rewritten from an older copy, and my entry was gone. I appended it again at 17:19 and it's there now, but it could be dropped again, and other sessions' entries may have been lost the same way. I couldn't tell which session did it. It's worth checking the log's diff before you commit.

**Where the change is:**
- **Unit tests:** built test-first, and 1,303 tests pass in my copy. I set aside four other sessions' unfinished test files, which don't compile yet. Every suite this change touches passed.
- **Lint:** swift-format and SwiftLint are clean on every changed file.
- **Not run:** the UI tests, the accessibility audits, coverage and the doc build. The audit test never got to run: the machine's load reached about 900, and every attempt timed out booting its simulator.

**Left before it can merge:**
- **A broken UI test:** UI-ONB-2 still calls the age-picker command I removed, so the UI test target doesn't compile.
- **Setting a date in UI tests:** the robots find elements by accessibility identifier, and it's still unknown whether that can set a date in the compact picker's pop-up. A small probe test for that is ready but hasn't run.
- **The accessibility audit:** the compact date picker hasn't passed the audit yet. It looks likely, because another session's compact time picker for bedtime passes. The docs' accessibility notes still describe the old wheel.
- **Landing it:** merge into the main tree, run the full test suite with coverage (the 80% check), and run the doc build.

**Decision for you:** a profile saved before this change holds only a birth year. As built, that birth year is dropped and the user picks their date of birth again. The alternative is converting it to an estimated date. Which do you want?

**Also:** you can't pick the exact date the picker suggests (a 30-year-old's birthday) straight away, because iOS only reports a change. Picking another date first works.

The work is in my session's scratchpad, in `work`. I also added two lessons to the parallel-sessions memory: boot simulators before running UI tests under this load, and set aside only other sessions' non-compiling tests.
